This file gathers in one place what waits on the owner and what a beta tester will meet. It restates its sources and does
not replace them: each row cites the document that holds the full text, and that document stays authoritative. To close
an item, record the owner's answer here and in its source, with the commit that applies it, and bump this file's rev and
revision log. "Ships today" is the behaviour of the tree at 7295f91b. A recommendation is the source's; since rev 7 a row says when the owner took it, which changed nothing in the tree;
"none recorded" means the source records none. Line numbers are as of 7295f91b and drift.

Short forms: *plan* = `tasks/api-fix-plan.md`; *ext design* = `tasks/extension-enable-design.md`; *lessons* =
`tasks/research/claude-code-design-lessons.md`; *storage design* = `tasks/storage-design/design.md`; *09*, *10*, *11*, *12*,
*03*, *08* = the files of `tasks/memory-redesign/` with those numbers; *hidden-chars* = `tasks/approval-hidden-chars.md`;
*programme plan* = `tasks/deps-upgrade/programme-plan.md` (the plan of record since 2026-10-20; rev 1.1 at this writing); *deps plan* =
`tasks/deps-upgrade/plan-rev3.md`, frozen at rev 3.18 — its section citations stay valid; *code audit* =
`tasks/code-audit-2026-07-19.md`.

`3598a6fb`), and `ZCode:<path>:<line>` cites ZCode (`/Users/tangyunpei/dev/AlpacaCode/ZCode`, Apache-2.0, read as
reference only). A row's existing recommendation is its source's and stays as recorded; an appended *ZCode (rev 5)*
sentence gives the audit's evidence and its recommendation in light of it — labelled "changes" where that differs
from the source's or gives one where none was recorded, "confirms" where it does not — and is never promoted to the
source's. Rows the audit raised carry its own ids, B-01…B-27 (its §4.4) and A-nn (its §4), which are neither the beta
gate's B1–B3 (§2) nor the 2026-10-17 review's B-nn (§3.10). OpenAlpaca code lines in rev 5's additions are the audit's
at `3598a6fb`, where nothing under `crates/`, `apps/`, `config/` or `scripts/` differs from `ff0e7740`.

stale once the records land; this file and each row's source are the record.

### 1.1 Tool governance and extensions

| ID | Question | Ships today | Answer (rev 7) and recommendation | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | A per-tool deny rule set (`[security.permissions] deny = [...]`, tool names and `<server>__*` globs, enforced in the gate on every surface, subagents and builtins included), apart from the per-server and per-plugin toggle? | No rule set. C8 purged the old per-tool deny key; a hand-edited `daemon.toml` still carrying it gets one boot WARN. The per-agent ALLOW axis is the only way to narrow tools. | **Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)**, the owner's blanket answer: no — no per-tool deny rule set; the two-axis rule and C8's purge stand, and if one is ever taken, deny is read before every allow path and mode shortcut (the ZCode sentence). Work: no change ships; the rejection is recorded as ext design §11.2 finding (4), written in that design's rev 18. *As recorded:* No. Also record the rejection as ext design §11.2 finding (4), which is not written yet. *ZCode (rev 5, confirms):* ZCode keeps a per-tool deny set and project allow/ask/deny rules (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/permission/service.ts:166-193), but its yolo mode allows before the deny list is read (:152-154 against :166). Work: not built; needs a plan entry, with T9. *As recorded:* `api_key_env` / `secret_ref` / `secret_encrypted` in that order, so the daemon stores a reference; decide with T9. | plan §0 T19 |

| T13 | Charge subagent spend to the lead's `max_cost` accumulator so "per-workflow" bounds the whole workflow? A new enforcement point beyond N4's relabel; pre-check (d), which would measure today's split, was never run (no provider keys). | The lead's cap ($5 default; the shipped `lead_agent` sets $3) bounds the lead's own calls; subagent spend shows in `get_task_usage` and is not counted; each subagent keeps its own $1 turn cap. | **Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)**, the owner's blanket answer: the relabel, as the ZCode evidence leans — `workflow_max_cost_usd` reads "lead agent spend", with the task-wide figure beside it; no workflow-scoped accumulator; N-LEAD-1 first. If no, relabel `workflow_max_cost_usd` "lead agent spend". *ZCode (rev 5, changes — leans to the relabel):* ZCode has no monetary budget; usage is summed over a run's lineage (ZCode:docs/dynamic-workflow/authoring.md:1291-1307), and a goal's token budget counts the parent session only (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/events/session.events.ts:1245-1258). If enforcement is wanted: a workflow-scoped accumulator checked at both loops' round boundaries, each loop's own cap unchanged and never summed into the other, the default re-derived from fan-out (8 × $1 already exceeds the shipped $3) and carried across resume. ### 1.6 Local model and first run

| ID | Question | Ships today | Answer (rev 7) and recommendation | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T20 | Should a first boot enable `[providers.ollama]` by itself when Ollama answers on `base_url` and no provider holds a key? | The seeded template ships `enabled = false`; with a running Ollama and no key the first turn fails `NoRoutableModel` until the owner flips one bit; the first-run card points to Settings → Models & keys. | **Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)**, the owner's blanket answer: keep `enabled = false` — enabling a provider is consent. Work: no change ships. *As recorded:* Keep `enabled = false`: enabling a provider is consent, and the cost is one documented action. *ZCode (rev 5):* ZCode has no local-model provider, so no evidence; keep `enabled = false` (audit §5.1). | plan §0 T20 |
| T24 | Offer the OAuth tokens found from Claude Code (`~/.claude/.credentials.json` or the Keychain item) and Codex (`~/.codex/auth.json`) on the GUI's first-run surface? | `GET /v1/settings/llm/credentials` and `…/rescan` exist and the GUI wraps them, but nothing calls them; metadata only. **The daemon discovers them already** (rev 3, found 2026-10-12): the seeded `llm.toml` has no `[credential_discovery]`, so both sources default on, and from boot and then every 30 s for the daemon's lifetime (`refresh_interval_secs`, default 30, `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/credential_discovery/mod.rs:119`) it reads `~/.codex/auth.json` and `~/.claude/.credentials.json` and, on macOS with the latter missing — as on the owner's machine — runs `security find-generic-password -s 'Claude Code-credentials' -w` against the login keychain, injecting any unexpired token into the router as a fallback key (in memory, never written). Whether that should stay on by default belongs to this decision (plan-rev3 rev 3.12, §5.2). | **Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)**, the owner's blanket answer: the ZCode form (B-18) — not offered; ship `[credential_discovery] claude_code = false, codex = false` in the seeded `llm.toml`, opt-in from Settings; `[cli_backends.*]`, "to be considered", not taken; were the feature ever offered, an explicit import with a consent card, a timeout and refusal as a stop. Work: not built; plan-rev3 §5.4's owner-decision table, B-18's row, after P38. *As recorded:* Not offered: it would show on screen that the machine is signed in elsewhere and use a credential minted for another product; it needs its own design with its own consent step. *ZCode (rev 5, changes — answers rev 3's sub-question; audit B-18):* ZCode imports another agent's configuration only from a user-opened import step, per item, never overwriting (ZCode:docs/onboarding-external-agent-import-steps.md:9-23), and never reads another agent's tokens; its one read of another product's secret is a user-started Chrome cookie import with a 15 s timeout that a denial ends (ZCode:packages/desktop/src/main/chromeCredentialManager.ts:6-10, :50-61); features that reach the network ship off unless the build enables them (ZCode:docs/desktop/build-time-optional-capabilities.md:5-11). In light of it (Z6-08, Z8-07): keep "not offered", and ship `[credential_discovery] claude_code = false, codex = false` in the seeded `llm.toml`, opt-in from Settings, considering the same for `[cli_backends.*]`; if the feature stays, an explicit import with a consent card, a timeout and refusal as a stop. Review SF-13 already uses exactly this pre-seed for live checks. | plan §0 T24 |

It narrows MD-10's trigger, so it stays the owner's (audit §5.1). | 09 §10 "Noted, not asked"; 03 §0 MD-10 |

Left to the lead, not the owner (recorded as measured; listed so they are not mistaken for settled; the owner's
blanket answer of rev 7 does not reach them):

- **Prose around an extraction answer.** `task_extraction::read_json_answer` takes the last value of the expected shape found in prose, so a complete quoted `{"items": [...]}` followed by a final non-items object is still taken. Yielding only the last value would close it. "Not decided in this batch; the reading is unchanged." (09 rev 3.34; 10 §9)
- **The 256-candidate cliff.** A word held whole in more than 256 eligible note bodies reaches the candidates only through the prefix query's 256, which can all be longer forms (`login` for `log`), losing every note that holds the word itself; no bench or proxy query shows it. A ranked body query beside the header query closes it at +11 ms p95 at 30K, +34 ms at 100K. (09 §6.5.4, §6.5.8; 10 §7.4, §9)
- **Header relevance's length bias.** It shrinks with a header's length; at 100K learned notes three identity questions (hq-0001, -0005, -0039) fall outside the 50 hits. Five variants were measured and each lost 1–4 proxy rows. (09 §6.5.5, §6.5.8; 10 §7.4, §9)

### 1.12 Raised by the ZCode audit (rev 5)

The audit (its §2, §3 and §4.4's *Owner decisions with a commit shape*) raised these, each because it adds an enforcement point, widens a component or reverses a settled ruling. The recommendation is the audit's in light of ZCode's evidence, or this revision's where the audit gives none (said so in the cell). **Since rev 7 all nineteen are taken** by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) — B-11's taken recommendation defers its cap value — and each row says where its work lands; until a commit builds one, the shipped behaviour is the "no change" answer. The audit's other decisions sit on existing rows — B-02 on T35, B-12 on T22, B-18 on T24, B-20 on OQ-2 — or in §4.3's ZCode table (B-22…B-25 and the programme-side questions). Its fixes within settled rules (the audit's N-LEAD-1…5, N-MCP-1…5, N-TOOL-1…7, N-SKILL-1…4, N-PLUG-1…6, N-DAEMON-1, N-STORE-1, N-GUI-1) are work, not decisions: plan-rev3 §5.4 (rev 3.14) and modernization §6.3 (rev 2.3) place them. *Rev 8:* nineteen of them, plan-rev3 §5.4's pre-cut entries, landed from `48f2b9a6` to `38649f7e` (the preamble's *Rev 8* paragraph names each with its commit); N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3…5, N-TOOL-3, N-TOOL-6, N-SKILL-3, N-PLUG-1, N-PLUG-3, N-PLUG-5 and N-GUI-1 are not built. Four owner questions those commits raised are §1.13's. *Rev 9:* B-11's cap value — 120 s by default, a 600 s ceiling, a model-settable `timeout_secs` clamped to it — and those four questions are taken by the owner's answer of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z); none is built (each row). *Rev 20:* N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3 and N-MCP-4 built (`f010f0a8`, `6e241978`, `1cd9dc26`), N-MCP-1's ledger half as N-MCP-1b (`8803771f`); N-MCP-5 not built.

Work: not built; plan-rev3 §5.4, after P38. *As recorded:* **Recommended: take the record's "idea for later" now.** `killpg` SIGTERM, then hand the unreaped child to a detached task that keeps `run_bounded`'s order (both pipes to their end, then wait), sends `killpg` SIGKILL after a 1–1.5 s grace if the child is still unreaped, and reaps it; the group id stays the child's until it is reaped, so R45's guarantee holds. **No** signal to enumerated descendant pids, as ZCode sends: that would signal pids the daemon neither owns nor holds unreaped and break R45's pid-reuse guarantee (`platform.rs:70-77`). Windows stays deferred (DU-33). It reverses R-T23. ### 1.13 Raised by the pre-cut fixes (rev 8)

The commits of rev 8 (the preamble's *Rev 8* paragraph) are fixes within settled rules, and each records in its body what it left for the owner. These four would add a bound, change every install's behaviour or widen a component, and no source records a recommendation, so each is **Still pending** and in §1's list. The first three are in the landing's report to the lead (a session scratchpad, not tracked; the commits are the record); the fourth is from `ab7a2e65`'s body alone. B-11's cap value, which N-TOOL-1 made decidable, stays on its own row (§1.12). *Rev 9:* all four are taken by the owner's answer of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) and have left §1's list; each row says what decided it and where its work lands, none built; so is B-11's cap value (§1.12).

|---|---|---|---|---|

The concurrency cap is unchanged, but nothing bounds how many subagents one run queues in total, each an instance, a lane and a task: the 30 s wait inside the lead's call that the commit removed was an accidental bound. | **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**: **a per-run lifetime backstop, refused with its number** — `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_subagents_per_run = 48` (= `max_concurrent_subagents` 6 × the batch cap 8; a derived default, re-derived if either moves). The 49th spawn of a run is refused to the lead with the figure; the lead prompt names the bound. The concurrency cap and N-LEAD-2's queueing are unchanged. One new key, one new refusal, a prompt line. *(decided by: Claude Code's **form** — a lifetime backstop far above normal use, its Workflow's 1,000 agents, "a runaway backstop" (this session's contracts); the **figure** is the lead's — OpenAlpaca pays a durable instance, lane and task row per queued subagent, which ZCode's in-memory map does not, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/runtime-task/registry.ts:112-126; ZCode has no bound by design, ZCode:docs/dynamic-workflow.md:237-238.)* Work: not built; scheduled after P38 as plan-rev3 §5.5's N-LEAD-6. *As recorded:* **Still pending** — none recorded; the commit keeps the cap as it was and names the gap. | `7461ab0b` (residue); plan-rev3 §5.4 N-LEAD-2; audit Z4-02 |

The registry still decodes nothing (N-TOOL-5's rule holds: nothing after the sanitizer decodes). This is the order N-TOOL-5's review *rejected the reverse of* (decode after the sanitizer); decoding before it is sound. One decode site in `SandboxManager`, one WARN; a test with a backtick, a line break, `../` and a NUL inside the string shows the sanitizer refusing the decoded object. ### 1.14 The subagent brief design (owner proposal, 2026-10-19) — SA-1…SA-4

The owner, in chat: "for the subagents which is used for the workflow … the orchestrator or the planner [writes] and then bring[s] up the subagents to work … a standard subagent template and let the leading agent inject and start the workflow, like what claude code does." The lead's reading, which the owner's answer takes: both references let the parent write the **prompt** and neither lets the model author the **tool set** at runtime (Claude Code: `Agent(subagent_type, prompt, model, …)` over `.claude/agents/*.md` definitions; ZCode: `Agent(description, prompt, subagent_type, run_in_background)` over disk profiles, `ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/tools/agent.ts:18-33`, and even its model-written workflow makes a `tools:` key a type error, `ZCode:docs/dynamic-workflow/authoring.md:113-149`). OpenAlpaca already has that shape (`spawn_subagent(agent_id, objective)`; the template fixes capabilities and caps — the settled "subagents stay template-scoped" holds). So the proposal is four separable changes, each **taken** on 2026-10-19 by the owner's rule, **none built**. None has a pending part, so §1's list gains no row: SA-2 is plan-rev3 §5.5's entry SA-2, and SA-1, SA-3 and SA-4 wait on a design to write first (§4.6).

| Id | Taken | Decided by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SA-1** | **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**: `general_agent` is the default when `agent_id` is omitted; the lead prompt stops matching "the best agent by skills" over nine personas and says: general unless the brief needs fewer powers (`explore_agent`, read-only and cheap) or more (`system_agent`); the specialist templates stay as capability profiles with shorter persona bodies. **Dependency:** L9's per-directory seeding gate means an existing install keeps its old templates, so SA-1 reaches installed stores only with **bundled defaults as the lowest-precedence scope** for agents and skills (Z3-06 and Z2-06's shape, Claude Code's: bundled definitions live in the binary, user files override, read at load) — a design to write; it changes ruling L9. Work: not built; needs a design — `tasks/subagent-brief-design.md`, after P38, before SA-1's commit (§4.6). | Claude Code (this session's contracts: three built-in types, `general-purpose` the default); ZCode agrees (`general-purpose` default, `ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/handlers/agent.ts:169`) | Z3-06 / Z2-06 (§4.7) are thereby **taken as SA-1's dependency**, a design to write — recorded in §4.7, no longer "record, do not schedule". |
| **SA-2** | **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**: the `objective` is written as a self-contained **brief** — goal, inputs, constraints, what to return and where — and the tool description and lead prompt say so. Nothing caps it (the 500- and 50-character cuts in `tools.rs:514`/`:850` are the span record and a log line). It stays the child's first user turn, never its system prompt — for a file-based (LLM) template; a plugin-backed template's executor is handed its objective appended to the instructions after the system prompt (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:909-914`), which SA-2 leaves as it is (plan-rev3 §5.5's SA-2). Work: not built; scheduled after P38 as plan-rev3 §5.5's SA-2. | Claude Code and ZCode alike (the prompt is the child's first user turn: `ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/runtime/methods/subagent.ts:454-461`) | A prompt-and-description commit; the cheapest of the four. |
| **SA-3** | **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**: templates stop pinning vendor model ids (all nine pin `claude-sonnet-4-6` or `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, unroutable on a local-only install, so every spawn today goes through the ladder's substitution): `model: inherit` (the routed model) is the default, with tier words the router resolves; **and** `spawn_subagent` gains a per-call `model` override, bounded to the router's routable models and still under the template's `max_cost_per_task`. Work: not built; needs a design — `tasks/subagent-brief-design.md`, after P38, before SA-3's commit (§4.6). | Claude Code (this session's contracts: definitions carry `model`; the Agent tool takes a per-call `model`) — ZCode deliberately removed the per-call override (`ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/tools/agent.ts:18-33`) and is not followed here by the owner's rule | Overrides the lead's lean (no per-call override). |

Work: not built; needs a design — `tasks/subagent-brief-design.md`, after P38, before SA-4's commit (§4.6). | Claude Code (this session's contracts: the Workflow tool's schema'd `agent()` returns a validated object); ZCode agrees (`ask<T>`, 3 repairs then `ValidationFailed` — `ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/dynamic-workflow/docs/execution-engine.md:216-256`) | **Reopens** the batch-work row taken 2026-10-17 as "no change; a lead-agent tool only after lead sequencing measurably fails" — reopened and taken by the owner's 2026-10-19 answer; recorded as such on that row (§1.11) and on Z4-16's §4.7 row. The schema must pass `strict_mode_compatible` before it is sent strict (CLAUDE.md, *Gotchas*). |

Not changed by the proposal: delegation depth 3 (`MAX_SUBAGENT_DEPTH`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:208`; a §6 option kept 2026-10-17), the per-template capability ALLOW, the cost caps, no script engine (the audit's call stands; Claude Code gates its own behind explicit opt-in), no per-workflow token ceiling (T13).

## 2. What a beta waits for

**Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**, the beta's scope: **the beta is cut from `main` after Part I merges** (plan-rev3 through P38/P41 — it clears the RustSec matches the review counted), with B1–B3; Parts II and III follow the beta. *(decided by: the lead.)* Work: scheduling only; nothing built. The beta also waits for B-22's notices commit, moved before it (after P38, before the beta is cut — it had stood beside Q09, in Part II; plan-rev3 §7.3's B-22 entry, `modernization-plan.md` §6.3's step 2a), and the LICENSE (plan-rev3 §5.1 item 16, an owner action) — the lead's placement ruling, 2026-10-19. *As recorded:* Which stages a beta waits for is the owner's call; Stages 0–2 are built. *(Rev 7: still pending — no recommendation recorded; §1's list.)*

Three things no stage covers (storage design §21):

1. **B1 — the branch reaches `main`.** Pushed: `origin/feat/markdown-memory` is at 7295f91b (checked 2026-10-11). PR #34
   is open and still a draft. It takes PR #34 leaving draft and being merged, and the beta build made from `main`,
   never from a working tree. **The owner** pushes and merges. *Rev 4:* PR #34 merged as `f253cc26` (2026-10-12 UTC),
   so the storage design is on `main`; the dependency programme's branch `chore/toolchain-and-deps` was cut from it and
   has never been pushed, and CI runs only on a pull request or a push to `main` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:3-6`), so
   that branch needs a draft PR of its own before its gates can be read (review SF-2; the owner actions under §4.3). *Rev 7:* unchanged by the owner's blanket answer — an owner action; the programme branch's push and draft PR
   are §4.3's first owner action. *Rev 13:* the lead's since the owner's ruling of 2026-10-27 (PDT), after P04 (§4.3). *Rev 14:* after P04b. *Rev 16:* done 2026-10-28 — pushed at `9d0a79d3`, one draft PR,
   tangyunpei/OpenAlpaca#35, its first CI run green; the merge stays the owner's.
2. **B2 — a fresh-install check of the packaged app**, after the last stage the beta includes, as 08 §4.1 did at
   `8fde2925`: the bundle from `scripts/release/package-macos.sh` on a clean home (`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` and
   `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` on a scratch directory, the daemon under `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` from its
   first launch, the local model), walking a tester's path — the first-run card; Ollama switched on or a key added from
   Settings; a streamed chat; an upload; a workflow that writes a file and a report naming its path; the memory round
   trip; an approval card answered; Stop and Start daemon; a second start on the same store — with the evidence
   archived beside 08's and the result in the PR. Not started; the last packaged check predates Stages 0–2. It is also
   the first live check of Stage 2's app surfaces and the stale approval card (`stage2-live-report.md` (i), vitest
   only so far) and of the four memory GUI fixes 08 found (`81912aba`, `69fe47d4`, `de2c409a`, `623bf13c`; 08 §4.1,
   §6), and the GUI process has never run under the no-network profile. It does not cover the Windows or Linux
   packages (§3.13). **Claude** can run it with screen access to the app, which the owner grants. *Rev 7:* unchanged — the
   screen access is the owner's to grant, the run Claude's.
3. **B3 — the manuals say what the beta cannot do.** One list in `docs/Installation_Manual.md`: the shell is not
   confined to the project (ST-14); extension tools never ask (T35); learning is below its own targets (10); a store
   from before 2026-09-28 is refused and the way through is the factory reset. Docs only, not started (no such list as
   of 2026-10-11). T35's answer decides its second line. **Claude** can write it; §3 below is its raw material. *Rev 5
   (audit A-41):* two more lines — a daemon the app spawns may lack the terminal's PATH, worked around with an absolute
   `command` or `env.PATH` in `mcp.toml` (Z6-02; §3.13, §4.3's *App PATH*), and the app is unsigned and not notarised
   (Z6-11, Z7-11; §3.13, §4.3's *Signing*). If taken, B-19's diagnostics bundle is named there too; under T35's option
   (iv) the second line would read "an extension tool that does not declare itself read-only asks".
   *Rev 7 (the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)):* the list describes the beta build,
   so a taken decision changes a line only once its commit lands. T35 is taken as option (iv): until B-02's commit
   (after P19) lands the second line reads as above; from it, "an extension tool that does not declare itself
   read-only asks, and an unattended turn refuses it". B-03, taken, reverses R30 for skills and is to land before B3
   is written. B-19's `openalpaca diag export`, taken, is named in the list once it is built. The PATH line stays —
   App PATH is taken as (a), the shell capturing the login environment once with (c) as its fallback, not built —
   and so does the unsigned line, for a first beta installed through the installer (Signing taken as (a), (b)
   before any wider distribution). Before any beta distribution, too: B-22's third-party notices (taken (b)) and the
   MIT LICENSE the manifests declare, which only the owner commits (§4.3's owner actions).

## 3. Limitations a beta tester meets

### 3.1 Shell and processes

- The shell is a working directory, not a confinement: an absolute path, `~` or `cd /` reaches anywhere, and an approved command runs as the user — it can read `state/discovery.json` and answer later prompts, edit `daemon.toml` to switch prompting off, and read the key file. The approval card is the only stop. Accepted for this version. (storage design §0.1 ST-14, §11)
- A shell command's changes keep no pre-edit copy; only `file_write` and `file_edit` keep one, so a command's damage cannot be undone from the conversation's log. (§18 Stage 2 item 13)
- A command that times out or is cancelled is killed with its process group by an immediate SIGKILL (R-T23), so it may leave its own lock files, such as `.git/index.lock`; the Daemon Manual says so. The idea for later is a SIGTERM grace with a detached reaper. (§18 Stage 2 item 9) *Rev 5:* the decision is B-07 (§1.12), recommended. *Rev 7:* B-07 taken — SIGTERM, a 1–1.5 s grace, a detached SIGKILL, no descendant signals (plan-rev3 §5.4, after P38); until built, still met.
- The kill does not reach everything: a process that leaves its group (`setsid`, a double fork) escapes; on Windows only the direct child dies; the daemon's forced exit kills nothing; a command in its own group no longer gets a terminal's Ctrl-C; a background command that reads `/dev/tty` stops until its time limit. (§18 Stage 2 item 8)

### 3.2 Files and the working folder

- Reading never asks: in a project an agent can read any file under its top folder, `.env` and other secrets included, and what it reads goes to the model. Accepted (ST-16); proven live. (storage design §0.1 ST-16; `stage2-live-report.md` (d))
- Replacing a file larger than 10 MB is refused, because its old content must be kept first. Accepted (ST-17).
- Inside a skill or a plugin agent, where no copy can be kept, `file_write` creates a new file but refuses to replace an existing one, and says why (R46).
- In a conversation with no project the model is offered the file readers only when the message says "read file", "open file", "show file" or "cat " — the open question is T37 (§1.5). (§18 Stage 2 item 28) *Rev 7:* T37 taken — `file_read` and `file_list` on every turn with a working folder, confined to the scratch folder and never asking; not built; until built, still met.
- Plugin agents and plugin skills get no working-folder line, so they are not told which folder the file tools and the shell act in. (§18 Stage 2 item 7)

### 3.3 Approvals

- An extension tool (MCP or plugin) asks only when it declares itself destructive; plugin tools never ask — the open question is T35 (§1.1). *Rev 5:* the audit recommends T35's new option (iv) (Z1-03). *Rev 7:* T35 taken as (iv), with B-01 (§1.1, §1.12; plan-rev3 §5.4, after P19); until built, still met, and B3's second line follows the build (§2).
- "Always allow" covers one tool for one subagent: each subagent of a workflow raises its own card for the same tool; a run-wide allow is not built (R43). (storage design §0.3, §12, §19) *Rev 5:* within that run it covers every later call of the tool whatever its arguments — one approval of `shell_execute ls` waives the card for `rm -rf` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/security/confirmation.rs:187-221`; audit Z1-02; the decision is B-01, §1.12). ZCode keeps R43's shape — a session-scoped allow covers only the subagent that asked (ZCode:docs/dynamic-workflow/launch.md:1060-1068) — so R43 stays, the fan-out pain answered by B-16 and B-01 (audit §5.3). *Rev 7:* B-01 ((a) and (b): the card declares per tool what Always allow covers, and a dangerous-command catalog asks every time) and B-16 (a prompt's clock starts at the head of its queue) are taken, R43 kept (§6); not built (plan-rev3 §5.4, after P38); until built, still met.
- *Rev 5:* a skill can remove prompts: its confirmation list replaces the defaults, its `skill_script:*` tools never ask, and the shipped create-skill lets a model write such a skill that the main loop's `invoke_skill` then runs, with no card at any step (audit Z3-01; the decision is B-03, §1.12). *Rev 7:* B-03 taken — the list adds to the defaults on all three skill paths and `skill_script:*` asks like the shell, cron skills narrower — to land before B3; until built, still met.
- *Rev 7:* a denial reaches the model as a bare refusal, so the model often retries the same call. B-10 is taken — an optional `feedback` on the denial, at most 4,096 characters, that the model reads (§1.12; plan-rev3 §5.4, after P38) — and not built; until built, still met.
- With no project, `file_write` asks for a by-product while `artifact_write` saving the same kind of file does not (ST-15's consequence; the `file_write` card seen live in (h), a by-product saved with no card in (e)). (storage design §0.5; `stage2-live-report.md` (e), (h))
- In Telegram, Discord and iMessage, `/yes` answers the chat's oldest pending prompt, keyed by chat and not by sender, so any participant of a group chat or channel can approve a tool (`crates/openalpaca_connectors/src/common/mod.rs:206`). Code audit H2 recommends binding a prompt to the person who raised it. A Discord preview line cut between a backslash and the character it escapes leaves that character bare. (§18 Stage 2 item 2; code audit H2; `tasks/patch-plan.md`, deferred) *Rev 5:* the decision is B-09 (§1.12). *Rev 7:* B-09 taken — both bindings, work and `/yes` only from the bound person — decided with MD-4, which records no recommendation and stays pending (§4.6); not built (plan-rev3 §5.4, after P38); until built, still met.
- The GUI draws integers past 2^53 in tool arguments rounded on approval cards, while the tool receives the exact value; the CLI and the connectors print exact digits. Fixing it is its own design. (hidden-chars, "Not taken in S3")
- Accepted hidden-character residue, recorded not to be re-raised: right-to-left letters reorder neutrals inside a value; the GUI's plain-text forms (the palette's Approve row, the Always-allow toast) can wrap inside an escape; the CLI reset does not reset the palette or the scroll region; a letter a particular font draws blank (U+FA2E/U+FA2F on macOS) cannot be closed by any set. (hidden-chars, accepted residue)
- *Rev 8:* the app's plugin manifest preview — the decision `approve` will be asked to make (`ManifestPreview`, `apps/openalpaca-gui/src/views/settings/AddExtensionForm.tsx:193`) — draws the manifest's name, description and declared capabilities unescaped, against the approval-surface escape rule (CLAUDE.md *Gotchas*). Pre-existing; found with N-PLUG-2 (`eb6b41fc`), whose own warning lines on that preview are escaped. Needs its own plan entry.

### 3.4 Refusals added in Stage 2's final wave

Each enforces a rule the design already had, but a person will meet it (storage design Part II, *New refusals the final wave added*):

- **Open on a result file** answers `409 OPEN_NOT_OFFERED` (Reveal only) unless the file its row resolves to is a regular file of a kind the store keeps — a folder, or a link to a script or an app, is refused (R-T25, ST-18; `open_asset_for_user`, `routes/files_types.rs:160`).
- **Open on a by-product** is decided on its stored file: a link out of its folder is refused (`500 OPEN_FAILED`, R-T33), a kind Open does not hand to an app is `409 OPEN_NOT_OFFERED`, and a `.bin` by-product (FW-1's fallback name) is not offered Open (`ArtifactRecord::openable`; `d01c9260`).
- **The run-end notifier** delivers to a chat app nothing `send` would refuse: a result file, another project's row, or a by-product whose file is not inside its own folder in the store (R-T3, R-T20; `deliverable_path`, `notification/artifacts.rs:87`).
- **An explicit confirmation list naming `file_write`** (a template's `require_confirmation_for`, a skill's `permissions.confirm.tools`) gets a card for `file_edit` too (R-T35; `effective_confirmation_set`, `security/sandbox/mod.rs:765`).
- **A missing project's result file** answers `410 ARTIFACT_PROJECT_MISSING` on Preview, Open and Export — the folder is gone or has moved, re-point it — and Reveal refuses with the same sentence (R-T26).
- **The file tools** refuse a leaf swapped to a link after the check (`O_NOFOLLOW` on unix) and a target that is not a regular file — a named pipe, a socket, a device — before any card (R-T30, X6; `open_leaf`, `tools/builtins/helpers/mod.rs:274`).

### 3.5 Memory and learning

All figures below are the local reader model grading itself (`qwen3.8:27b-mlx`, LQ1), one sample of a live system each. Recall from saved notes passed its gate: D-index +6.6 pp over the old engine, CI [+3.7, +9.5] (10 §1).

- Learned memory answers below curated notes: 88.1 % (414/470) against 96.4 %, −8.3 pp, CI [−11.3, −5.3]; the CI misses the −10 pp target, and cross-language (−12.9 pp) and rejected or failed actions (−13.3 pp) fall below the block line. No re-target or re-measurement is recorded. (10 §1, §3.4, §8)
- Other-script aliases on learned notes (what makes cross-language recall work): median 56.1 % against a proposed 60 %; LQ18 keeps 60 % a target, not a gate. The gap sits on three replayed sessions, the build's extraction is likely not the cause, and the figures were not re-scored under LQ18. (10 §4.2, §4.8, §9; 09 §10 LQ18)
- Changed facts: 10 of 15 against 12 (`user.weekly_review` wrong in every final run). A suggestion was raised for 7 of 8 changed saved notes; the commute was missed because the reader stored the new value as a second active note beside the saved one. LQ19 answers the commute; it is built and unmeasured. (10 §4.2, §4.4, §4.5, §9)
- Scale: at the 30K design point recall p95 is 81.8 ms but a learned store takes 189.6 ms, an edit 252.2 ms and a forget 356.4 ms, with no write-path target set. At 100K recall p95 is 261.3 ms, a learned store about 3.1 s, and the 100K bench's peak RSS about 2.1 GB, a rise nobody has explained. A missing index rebuilds in the background (4.2 s at 30K, about 17 s at 100K), during which search answers from a file scan and `memory_timeline` is refused. Accepted by LQ15 (design point about 30K). (10 §7.2, §7.4, §9)
- Continuity: a new conversation answers questions about the previous one at 38/60 = 63.3 % against 80 %, +10.0 pp over carry-over off against +30 pp; `doing` + `decided` 36/40, `next` 2/20. Open findings: the writer puts the daemon's conditions (a refused approval, the workflow cap) into `next` (12 §6.2), and a line it reads cut looks unfinished (§6.3); the carry-over folding into the next conversation's state (§6.1) was answered by C1, and C1 and C2 were built after and not re-measured. The owner accepted `next` as measured on 2026-09-30 (`tasks/todo.md:112`; CLAUDE.md); 11, 12 and plan §0 do not record it. (12 §1, §3.2, §6, §7; 11 rev 1.8) *Rev 5:* ZCode marks a daemon-made input with its source and a model-only visibility that every user-facing projection filters (ZCode:docs/subagent-respond-to-coordinator.md:24-48; the audit's † answer, verified here). In light of it: label whole daemon-authored rows — no-answer lines, completion reports — `[daemon]` for the working-state writer and re-measure on 12's split (audit Z4-13; lines appended inside an assistant row, such as RunClaimGuard's, not covered); reopening `next` is the owner's. *Rev 7:* the labelling and the re-measure are taken as the ZCode sentence recommends (Z4-13, §4.7) — not built; needs a plan entry (design 11's working-state writer, 12's split run again); reopening `next` stays the owner's, no recommendation recorded (§1's list).
- Episode notes (conversation summaries) are unverified on this build: CQ-11 restored the call that answered 17 of 20 on the L8 build (scored before eq-017's gold was corrected), and nothing has been re-run since. (12 §5, §8; 10 §5; 11 §10.3)
- Memory search is lexical: no embedding and no model. A term inside a word of a space-separated body is not found — `book` in `runbook`, `wechsel` in `Zeitzonenwechsel`, `3.12` in `python3.12`, `4090` in `RTX4090`. Aliases are the remedy, by design (MD-1). (03 §0 MD-1; 09 §0.3, §6.5.4–§6.5.5)

### 3.6 Connectors

- iMessage runs every accepted message as the Mac owner: `resolve_owner` ignores the sender (`imessage/handler.rs:219`), a direct message from a handle not in `owner_handles` is processed once it starts with the trigger prefix (`imessage/routing.rs:132-155`), and group messages follow the prefix setting. Code audit H1, "security-critical": a per-sender principal with a linked-identity check. (code audit H1; `tasks/patch-plan.md`, deferred) *Rev 5:* the decision is B-09 (§1.12). *Rev 7:* B-09 taken (both bindings), decided with MD-4, which stays pending; until built, still met.
- A connector lane (`{owner}:{platform}`) mixes every DM and group on the platform and learns and recalls as an app lane does, so the owner's notes can be recalled in a group chat. Group privacy is its own unwritten design (MD-4; §4.6). (03 §0 MD-4, §3 rule 5; 09 §0.3) *Rev 7:* MD-4 still pending, no recommendation recorded (§1's list).
- `/yes` per chat, not per sender: §3.3.
- Plugin connectors do not work: the bridge `get_plugin_connector` has no production caller and `ConnectorHandle::Plugin` is built nowhere. (CLAUDE.md, *Extensibility*) *Rev 8:* since `eb6b41fc` (N-PLUG-2) a manifest declaring `types.connector` validates, installs and lists with a warning that connectors are not supported — on the validate dry run, the install and update preview, the listed row awaiting consent, `ext info` and the log; deleting the bridge is taken (§4.3's *Plugin connector bridge*) and needs a plan entry.

### 3.7 Models and the LLM router

- The streaming path, which every main-loop turn takes, bypasses the circuit breaker and the per-key RPM/TPM/concurrency limiter; CLAUDE.md's Router Flow steps 5–7 describe the non-streaming path. Q3 (§1.9). (code audit M3) *Rev 7:* Q3 taken — one acquire/report path for streaming, retrying or answering `EmptyCompletion` only before the first delta; not built; until built, still met.
- One circuit breaker serves every provider, so a dead Ollama blocks Anthropic too, and a HalfOpen breaker is not refused. Q4, Q5 (§1.9). (code audit M5) *Rev 7:* Q5 taken — one breaker per provider — not built, so until built still met; Q4, a HalfOpen breaker refused, still pending, no recommendation.
- Per-key `allowed_models` and `monthly_budget` do not exist; they were removed, never enforced. (Vault 11; `tasks/wiring-audit-2026-08-30.md` §4)
- Plugin LLM providers do not work: `get_plugin_provider` has no production caller; D-I of the paused LLM design removes the switch with one install warning, unbuilt. (CLAUDE.md, *Extensibility*; `tasks/llm-simplify/design.md` D-I) *Rev 8:* that warning is built for providers by N-PLUG-2 (`eb6b41fc`): a manifest declaring `types.provider` is told the plugin provider bridge is not wired, on the same surfaces as the connector's (§3.6); the switch itself is not removed.
- *Rev 8:* a refused key is named: when every attempt was refused credentials (a 401/403, `AuthenticationFailed`) the router answers `AllKeysRefused`, naming Settings → Models & keys and `openalpaca llm keys add`, never retried, the fallback ladder still walked — where it used to end as `AllFallbacksFailed`, retried three times as transient (`6423a084`). Under `SelectionStrategy::PrimaryFallback` an auth error puts no key into cooldown, so the pool may offer the primary on every attempt and never try the fallback key before that answer — as before (`6423a084`'s residue).

### 3.8 Extensions

- MCP resources and prompts are not implemented, and serving MCP is a non-goal: OpenAlpaca imports MCP tools only. (CLAUDE.md, *Extensibility*)
- Plugin-contributed skills cannot stream and cannot take attachments: the protocol returns a finished answer and takes a query string, so the turn gets one fallback delta and every attachment is recorded skipped. (CLAUDE.md, local-model rounds 7 and 9)
- The MCP HTTP transport has no redirect policy (`openalpaca_mcp` `transport/http.rs`), re-verified 2026-10-10; "recommended soon as its own fix". (deps plan §3.3) *Rev 4:* the 2026-10-17 review found it scheduled nowhere and that an API-key header under any name and every `extra_headers` entry follow a cross-origin redirect (reqwest strips only Authorization, Cookie and Proxy-Authorization); rmcp's fix (GHSA-9g45-5xwm-f3wc) covers rmcp's own client only. Whether to refuse cross-origin redirects is **D6** (§4.3), pending. (review SF-6) *Rev 7:* D6 taken as (a), with its sub-question answered yes — cross-origin redirects refused, and every redirect when `auth` or `extra_headers` are configured, as a typed, non-retriable error naming the target origin — in N-MCP-2's commit, before the cut; until it lands, still met. *Rev 8:* built in N-MCP-2's commit `48f2b9a6`, D6 (a) and its third form as taken: a hop to another origin is refused for every server and any hop for a server with `auth` or `extra_headers`, an unauthenticated server following same-origin hops up to reqwest's ten; each refusal is the new `McpError::RedirectRefused { origin }`, naming the origin only, not retriable — a call returns it at once and never reconnects, and at bring-up the row reads `Failed{ConfigInvalid}` with it in the detail; `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s MCP section says so. No longer met. What a tester meets instead: a server whose `url` relies on a redirect, http→https included, stops connecting until `url` names the endpoint it redirected to, and the model is told so in `ConfigInvalid`'s shared sentence ("could not be parsed … the file needs repair", action `Repair`), pinned by ext design §7.1's table row — its own wording needs a plan entry (§5).
- *Rev 5 (audit Z5-02, Z5-03):* an MCP call that times out is sent again after a reconnect, so a non-idempotent tool can run twice (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:552-584`), and the MCP HTTP client's hard-coded 30 s total timeout cuts every streamed body — SSE answers, the legacy GET stream, P19b's listen stream (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/http.rs:96-98`). Both are fixes (plan-rev3 §5.4): N-MCP-2 before the cut; N-MCP-1 after G-base's run of record and before P19, at Phase G's head after P18, because `step-6-rmcp.patch` rewrites its region of `client.rs` — folded into M15 only if P19's rewrite over it proves costly, never dropped. *Rev 7:* T6(a) is taken in the ZCode form N-MCP-1 carries out; the ledger's timing stays pending (§1.1). *Rev 8:* N-MCP-2 landed as `48f2b9a6`: the streamable-HTTP client keeps a connect bound (the shorter of 30 s and the request timeout) and an idle bound only (the longer of five minutes and the configured request timeout, reset by every byte), a call's deadline staying `McpClient`'s `request_timeout` — the 30 s total cut is gone, and a stream held open between events is not cut for silence. N-MCP-1 is not built, so a timed-out call can still be sent again after a reconnect; residue in §5. *Rev 9:* T6(a)'s ledger timing is taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) — a stdio child's exit reads `Failed` at once, its detail the exit code or signal, recovery unchanged (§1.1); not built, N-MCP-1 amended. *Rev 20:* built — N-MCP-1 (`f010f0a8`): a call that may have reached the server is never sent again, one deadline over the pre-send reconnect and the call; N-MCP-1b (`8803771f`): a stdio exit reads `Failed` at once and the next call's reconnect recovers it (§1.1).
- *Rev 7:* two more limitations that taken decisions will remove, neither built — until built, still met: a mid-session 401 from an HTTP MCP server fails the server at once, a rotated `bearer_env`/`api_key_env` taking effect only at `reload` or `enable` (T7, taken: one re-resolve and one retry, for a 401 on a request that carried the env credential, §1.1); and one bad server block fails the whole `mcp.toml`, an unparseable file booting with no servers (B-17, taken: the per-block unknown-key warning, then a per-block parse, §1.12).
- *Rev 5 (audit Z2-01):* a plugin's skill id, slash or agent id that a shipped one already holds replaces it, and the plugin's next disable deletes both. The fix is N-PLUG-6 (plan-rev3 §5.4); the skip rule is B-05 (§1.12). *Rev 7:* B-05 taken — the skip rule on both insert paths, after N-PLUG-6; until built, still met. *Rev 8:* N-PLUG-6 landed as `1a5512dc`: a plugin naming a taken template id gets a log warning and does not take it, and an unload removes only what the plugin's own load registered, never through the name scan, so a shipped template or a file skill is no longer deleted at the plugin's disable. A plugin skill still inserts over a file skill's key until B-05's skip rule, not built (§1.12).
- *Rev 5 (audit Z1-06 = Z5-11):* every MCP stdio server and plugin inherits the daemon's whole environment, every credential it received through it included, less `OPENALPACA_MASTER_KEY`. The decision is B-04 (§1.12). *Rev 7:* B-04 taken in its minimal form — every `secret_env` name, every `mcp.toml` `*_env`/`*_from` name and `OPENALPACA_*` removed at spawn (after P19, and in `process_pool.rs`); until built, still met.

### 3.9 Store, data and retention

- A store from before 2026-09-28 (schema 42–45) reads as newer than this build and is refused with nothing changed; the way through is `openalpaca config reset --factory`, which deletes the database and `llm.toml`'s keys. Keep a copy of `state/openalpaca.db` first, and never start a pre-squash build against the new store. (`tasks/store-revision/design.md` §0 SQ-1, §1.4, §5; storage design §21 B3)
- `event_log` grows forever: every `ServerEvent` but three is persisted, plus the sandbox's rows, and only a factory reset or a project purge removes any. (CLAUDE.md, *Gotchas*, W6) *Rev 5:* ZCode names its transient event types in one set and evicts them, keeping audit events (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/events/session-event-retention.ts:9-25); still nobody's default — if wanted, the shape is the audit's Z8-05 (§4.7). *Rev 7:* still nobody's default — Z8-05 records no recommendation and stays a later design (§4.7).
- Session logs are bounded by size only; `log_retention_days` is inert. T12 (§1.4). *Rev 7:* T12 taken — 90 days with the age sweep built, `persist` reserved as a direction; the default stays 0 and inert until the sweep's commit, which needs a plan entry; until built, still met.
- *Rev 5 (audit Z8-03):* every Open of an upload copies it to `$TMPDIR/openalpaca-open/<id>-<name>` and nothing removes the copy (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/files_types.rs:66-75`); the CLI's editor buffers sit in `$TMPDIR` too, and neither a project's Delete data nor the factory reset reaches them (recorded in `tasks/storage-design/01-current-layout.md:105-106`). The fix is N-STORE-1 (plan-rev3 §5.4): both under `state/`. *Rev 8:* N-STORE-1 landed as `38649f7e`: Open stages its copy at `state/cache/open/<file id>/<name>` in 0700 folders, the daemon's boot sweeps every staged copy before any route serves, a project's Delete data removes the copies of the uploads it deletes, and the CLI's editor buffers (`memory add`, `memory edit`, `persona edit user`) live in `state/editor/` at 0700, which the factory reset leaves and its preview lists. No longer met. A `$TMPDIR/openalpaca-open/` an earlier build left is not swept (the OS ages it out); residue in §5.
- *Rev 7:* an uninstalled plugin's tree stays in `plugins/.trash/<name>-<ts>/` until removed by hand, as the CLI manual says; T17 is taken as no auto-purge, so this stays as it is (§1.5).
- The app's own webview storage sits outside the store: §3.12 (B-27).

### 3.10 Security

- `web_fetch`'s SSRF check does not resolve hostnames (`validate_url`, `tools/url_validation.rs:39`, synchronous; redirects re-checked the same way), so a name resolving to a private or metadata address passes. Code audit M10: validate every resolved IP. (code audit M10) *Rev 5:* ZCode blocks only literal private addresses, with no DNS, to stay within a 1 s budget (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/handlers/webfetch-egress-guard.ts:14-27); M10's check of every resolved IP stays the stronger answer — resolve once and connect to the checked address (audit §5.3).
- HEAD's locks carry 5 RustSec vulnerabilities — lopdf (a crafted PDF aborts the daemon; reachable), rustls (reachable), quick-xml ×2, RUSTSEC-2026-0194/0195 (likely reachable), rmcp (server transport, not compiled) — and 24 `bun audit` advisories; zip2's three GHSAs touch `extract()` paths the daemon does not call. The dependency wave clears them (measured 5→0, 24→0); DU-1's option (C) is a security slice first if an untrusted PDF or spreadsheet upload reaches anyone sooner. (deps plan §0 item 2, §4.1 DU-21, §4.2 DU-1) *Rev 4 — the count is stale:* against RustSec advisory-db `64f92cd` (2026-10-16T23:27Z) HEAD's lock matches **14** RustSec vulnerabilities, not five — the extra nine quinn-proto 0.11.17's RUSTSEC-2026-0338/-0340/-0343 (P04 clears them), rustls 0.23.43's -0346/-0348/-0349 and zip 2.4.2's -0367/-0368/-0369 — and unsafe-libyaml carries RUSTSEC-2024-0449 (unmaintained). Four rustls advisories published 2026-10-14 (-0346…-0349, all patched in 0.23.46) are not cleared by P04's 0.23.45, which introduces -0347; -0349 reaches every HTTPS client in the daemon. Whether to waive the 7-day rule for rustls 0.23.46 or wait is **D2** (§4.3); the zip advisories need P34's ignore active, because calamine keeps zip 8.6 in the lock. (review MF-3, SF-7, B-09) *Rev 6 — D2 settled, and the cut-day count measured:* D2 is taken and covered by D1 (a) (§4.3), so P04 takes rustls 0.23.46 and clears -0346…-0349. The "measured 5→0" above no longer holds: against advisory-db `64f92cd` the cut-day lock matches **3** RustSec vulnerabilities, not 0 — RUSTSEC-2026-0367/-0368/-0369 on zip 8.6.0, which calamine 0.36.1 (the newest release, requiring `zip ^8.6`) keeps, on `extract()` / `ZipStreamReader` paths neither calamine nor our code calls, and patched only in zip ≥ 9.0.0, so no eligible release fixes them; P34 and E2 carry them as three reasoned ignores, and cargo-audit's configuration the same, until a calamine release requires zip ^9 (compatibility report C6). `bun audit` on the cut-day JS set: 0 in 203 packages. (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md` §1, §3.6, C6) *Rev 22:* unsafe-libyaml and its RUSTSEC-2024-0449 left the lock with serde_yaml at P30 (`f0fc0436`; cargo-audit 3 vulnerabilities, warnings 8 → 7), and `bun audit` is 0 since P32 (`093966fd`: 7 → 0; 42 at P31's parent), 0 in 203 packages at P33 (`0286a80f`).
- Also under §1: keychain secrets shared across roots (T10), the web-search key in plaintext (T19), the unscoped confirmations read (T22); and iMessage and per-chat `/yes` above. *Rev 7:* T10, T19 and T22 are taken — keychain accounts scoped by `install_id`; `[web_search]` under the secret resolution order; the confirmations read scoped, answering by id kept — and none is built; until built, each is still met.

### 3.11 Tasks and routing

- **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z)**, `/pause`: **report "pause unsupported" now** — `/pause` and `/resume` answer one sentence (pause is not supported; `/cancel` stops a run) and **change no status** (today `/pause` flips the row while execution and spend continue). No pause token. A later design, when a cancelled run can be resumed, maps `/pause` to cancel-as-interrupted plus resume. *(decided by: Claude Code (this session's contracts) — no pause: Esc interrupts, `TaskStop` stops background work; ZCode agrees — no pause for a workflow run, stop → `stopped`, resume replays the journal, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/dynamic-workflow/src/engine/types.ts:492,500.)* Work: not built; the two commands' deterministic answers in `task_ops.rs`, scheduled after P38 as plan-rev3 §5.5's N-ROUTE-1; until built, still met. *As recorded:* `/pause` only changes a run's status; execution and spend continue, since nothing in the agentic loop checks a pause (`orchestrator/task_ops.rs`). Code audit M16: honour a pause token or report "pause unsupported"; Routing V2 §3: a pause check at the drain seam, or document `/pause` as advisory. (code audit M16; `tasks/design-routing-v2.md` §3) *Rev 5:* ZCode has no pause: a stop leaves a run resumable and resume replays its journal (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/dynamic-workflow/docs/execution-engine.md:513-581; the audit's † answer, verified here). In light of it: report "pause unsupported", or map `/pause` to cancel-as-interrupted plus replay; no pause token (audit §5.3). *Rev 7:* still pending — no one recommendation recorded (§1's list).
- The CLI and the GUI share one lane, so a CLI turn from another project can switch the conversation an open GUI window shows. T18 (§1.3). *Rev 7:* T18 taken — the shared lane kept and documented; a lead-queued follow-up pinned to `task.session_id` now (not built; needs a plan entry); the GUI's own session pinned only if archiving proves disruptive — so the CLI's switch of the GUI's conversation stays as it is.
- *Rev 5 (audit Z4-01):* when a lead's run ends with subagents still running — its round or cost cap, or the 600 s wait cap — nothing cancels them: the run's token is removed without being cancelled (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/orchestrator/dispatcher/lead_agent.rs:1068`), so they keep calling the model outside every cap, writing files and raising cards, and `/cancel` cannot reach them. The fix is N-LEAD-1 (plan-rev3 §5.4), first in the lead set; B-15 (§1.12) is the option on top. *Rev 7:* B-15 taken with its condition — N-LEAD-1's cancel only, a completion guard only if runs are seen abandoning work they could have collected — and B-13 (`cancel_subagent`, then `message_subagent`) after N-LEAD-1 (§1.12). *Rev 8:* N-LEAD-1 landed as `f6c11897`: when the lead's loop returns, its subagents' token is cancelled (R45's process-group kill of a running command rides on it), each subagent is given 5 s to close its lane as cancelled — "the run ended before this subagent finished" — what still runs is aborted, and the report and the summary end with a line naming the stopped lanes. No longer met. B-15 (1) is that cancel (§1.12); B-13 and T13's relabel can be scheduled, neither built; residue in §5.
- *Rev 8:* a spawn no longer waits: since `7461ab0b` (N-LEAD-2) a subagent past the lead's bound is queued and its spawn returns its run id at once, where before entries 7 and 8 of a full batch on a stock install (bound 6) each held the lead 30 s and then failed unless a slot freed. A queued subagent's instance reads busy and its lane `running` in the timeline (the span schema has no queued state; adding one is a migration). Nothing bounds how many subagents one run queues, and on a one-key install a sixth subagent reads `running` while it waits inside the router — both §1.13's. *Rev 9:* both taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) — a per-run backstop of 48 subagents (N-LEAD-6), and per-key concurrency 8 with `docs/agent-loop.md` documenting the `running` read (N-LLM-1) (§1.13); not built; until built, still met.
- *Rev 7:* the workflow cap bounds the lead's own calls only: subagent spend shows in `get_task_usage` and is not counted, each subagent keeping its own $1 turn cap. T13 is taken as the relabel — `workflow_max_cost_usd` read as "lead agent spend", the task-wide figure beside it, no workflow-scoped accumulator, N-LEAD-1 first — and not built; until built, the cap is still described as per-workflow (§1.4). *Rev 8:* N-LEAD-1 is built (`f6c11897`); the relabel is not, and still needs a plan entry.
- *Rev 7:* a follow-up a crash left `running` is never settled. B-14 is taken — boot settles it `cancelled`, at most once, with a notice — after N-DAEMON-1 (plan-rev3 §5.4); not built; until built, still met (§1.12). *Rev 8:* N-DAEMON-1 is built (`08831341`): a run that finishes during a shutdown no longer starts the lane's next follow-up, which stays `queued`, so a stop no longer strands a row `running`; a crash mid-turn still does, and B-14 is not built.

B-27 records that exception to D1 in CLAUDE.md's D1 bullet and the manuals, in P38's commit; N-GUI-1, after Q07, namespaces the `oa-*` keys by the served store, so a different store starts empty (plan-rev3 §5.4). ### 3.13 Packaging and platforms

- Only the macOS bundle has been packaged and checked (08 §4.1). The `windows-latest` CI job runs only the Pester suite, CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri`, whether the workspace builds on Windows or on a clean Ubuntu without OpenSSL headers is unanswered, and no supported OS or webview floor is written anywhere. (storage design §21 B2; deps plan Appendix B #9, #28, DU-18, DU-31) *Rev 7:* DU-33 (a) stands — Q08's Windows and Linux legs are not written now (*DU-33 (a) reopened*, taken as (a)); the macOS floor is taken as 13.3 ((c)-2) and the macOS CI job as (b) (§4.3).
- The release archive ships two different `openalpacad` builds of one commit — `libexec/openalpacad` and the app's sidecar — because the scripts build the CLI and the daemon jointly; and `prepare-sidecar.ts`'s freshness check compares a `tauri-build` copy with itself. Until DU-30's Q02/Q03, build the daemon alone, once, and hash it before `tauri build`. (deps plan §3.3; 08 §4.1, §6)
- A scratch store (`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE`) does not isolate `$HOME`: with the seeded `llm.toml` the daemon runs credential discovery and detects the Claude Code CLI at boot, so an isolated run that may reach a model must switch both off. (08 §4.1, §6) *Rev 4:* the 2026-10-17 review found the switch-off unspecified in the plan: the seed template has no `[credential_discovery]` or `[cli_backends]` section, discovery runs unless both sources are explicitly false (`apps/openalpacad/src/services/llm.rs:178`), and with no `[cli_backends]` the router registers whatever `which claude` / `which codex` finds. The exact pre-seed and a log check belong in deps plan §5.2. (review SF-13) *Rev 7:* T24 is taken — discovery seeded off (`[credential_discovery] claude_code = false, codex = false`), opt-in from Settings (B-18; plan-rev3 §5.4, after P38); until built, every isolated run still seeds both sources and the CLI backends off (deps plan §5.2), and `[cli_backends.*]`'s seed is left open.
- Starting the daemon from the app (Settings → Connection → `Start daemon`) spawns the bundled sidecar with no sandbox profile (`startDaemon`, `apps/openalpaca-gui/src/lib/daemon-control.ts:146-151` → `ensure_daemon_running`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:39-52` → `spawn_daemon`, which sets only the config-dir and managed-log variables, `:343-350`). A run that keeps the daemon confined by starting it first under `sandbox-exec` loses that confinement at the first in-app Start. (review MF-4; `tasks/deps-upgrade/baselines/2026-10-12/README.md`)
- *Rev 5 (audit A-41, Z6-11, Z7-11):* the app is unsigned and not notarised, so Gatekeeper warns of an unidentified developer unless the quarantine flag is removed, which the installer does with `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` (`scripts/release/install.sh:376-378`; the packaging script's comment says the bundle is not codesigned, `scripts/release/package-macos.sh:14-15`); whether to sign is §4.3's *Signing* row. *Rev 7:* Signing taken as (a) for a first beta installed through the installer, said in B3; (b), signed and notarised, before any wider distribution, with the owner's Apple Developer account.
- *Rev 5 (audit Z6-02):* a daemon the app spawns inherits the app's environment, and an app opened from Finder gets launchd's minimal PATH (reasoned, not measured), so `npx`, `uvx`, Homebrew's `node` and `claude` may be missing for it while present for `openalpaca daemon start`; the workaround is an absolute `command` or `env.PATH` in `mcp.toml`; the decision is §4.3's *App PATH* row. *Rev 7:* App PATH taken as (a) with (c) as its fallback — not built; Q21 (plan-rev3 rev 3.16), after Q14 and before Q04; until built, still met, and B3 carries the limitation.
- *Rev 5 (audit Z7-02):* no LICENSE file is in the tree, although `Cargo.toml:40` and `apps/openalpaca-gui/package.json:6` declare MIT, and neither the bundle nor the archive carries third-party notices (B-22, §4.3). *Rev 7:* B-22 taken as (b) — the owner commits the MIT LICENSE (§4.3's owner actions), `deny.toml`'s licence policy lands in P34, and THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES are generated from both lockfiles and bundled before any beta distribution; until then, still met.
- *Rev 5 (audit Z7-01):* CI never compiles the macOS-only code — the `getattrlistbulk` scan, iMessage, keychain discovery (§4.3's *macOS CI job*). *Rev 7:* taken as (b) — a `rust-macos` PR check on a pinned label, (c) (schedule and dispatch only) if the minutes matter — Q20 (plan-rev3 rev 3.16), before Q08; until it lands, still met.
- *Rev 22 (P32, `093966fd`):* a developer's test run writes a token outside the repository and `~/.openalpaca`: Vitest 5.0.4's `fsModuleCache`, on by default, writes `<repo root>/node_modules/.vitest-cache` (ignored by `.gitignore`'s `node_modules/`) and a `.vitest-secret-token` under `$HOME/Library/Application Support/vitest`, so `bun run test` leaves a token under the real `$HOME`; P32's gates ran with a scratch `HOME`. (programme plan §3.2 *P32*)

## 4. Planned work that is paused or never started

### 4.1 LLM providers as a table — paused in section 1 of 5

Users would add any OpenAI- or Anthropic-compatible service from Settings, the CLI or `llm.toml`, the three built-ins
becoming rows of one table; D-H…D-N were taken on 2026-09-23. Nothing is implemented or deleted and there is no spec;
the survey maps `map-construction.md`, `map-identity.md` and `map-wire.md` were never written and must be regenerated
before section 2. To resume: re-present section 1 with its open question (§1.9, *no-key-free*), then sections 2–5 with
the owner's approval after each; the deps plan says land the dependency wave first (it overlaps P11, P12, P29).
(`tasks/llm-simplify/design.md`, *Where to resume*; `tasks/todo.md`, Chunk 24; deps plan §3.4)

*Rev 7:* section 1's open question, *no-key-free*, and *id-collision* are taken (§1.9: one rule, unpriced until
a model's prices are set to 0; the registry and selection keyed by `(provider, model)`), neither built. Whether and
when to resume the design stays the owner's — no recommendation recorded (§1's list).

Unchanged: research 2.0 first, adding `flatten` under serde-saphyr, the default-on comments feature, and fresh fixtures for a BOM, CRLF, `>`/`>-`/`\|`/`\|+`, a quoted value containing `: `, a nested map, an unknown key and an empty body (audit A-07). *Evidence (rev 6; the cut-day compatibility report §5 — evidence, not a decision):* serde-saphyr 2.0.0 (2026-10-13T15:11:53Z, rust-version 1.89, the only 2.x, with granit-parser 2.0.0) is eligible at D1 (a)'s cut with no step-back and no RustSec advisory, and only `openalpaca_core` depends on it. As a drop-in it compiles and passes `middleware::skill`'s 20 tests with one rustc `deprecated` warning, which G-lint's 0-`deprecated` rule refuses: `reject_non_finite_typeless_float` gives way to `non_finite_float_policy: serde_saphyr::NonFiniteFloatPolicy::AsString`, its exact equivalent (serde-saphyr 2.0.0's `src/de/cfg.rs:44-48`). With that one line the core suite keeps the 1.3 baseline's counts, the workspace is 5,126 / 0 / 17 in the Rust leg's variant and clippy 0; on the four repository skills every facet is identical to 1.3's, and on 42 synthetic frontmatters 1.3 and 2.0 accept and reject the same inputs with equal values — only error texts differ, 2.0's cleaner; E6's `yamlcmp` rebuilt against `=2.0.0` gives 1.3's verdicts. Two fixes apply to P30 under either answer, being 1.3's gaps too: **C1** — `emit_comments: false` and a pin test, because 1.3, and 2.0 with its default `comments` feature, refuse a frontmatter with 33 or more consecutive comment lines between a key and its block value, which serde_yaml 0.9 reads (none of the four repository skills has one; the owner's real skills are E6's to check); **C10** — the doc comment saying a `serde_json::Value` position gets the canonical `.inf`/`.nan` spelling. The report found no blocker and takes no decision: under (a) P30 writes `serde-saphyr = "2.0"` with those options, under (b) 1.3.0 with them and the hold named with 2.0.0 as its way out. (Rev 6: then still pending.) *Rev 11:* the tool halves are built — E6's `yamlcmp` on `serde-saphyr = "=2.0.0"` with `non_finite_float_policy: AsString` and `emit_comments: false` (`2c913f99`, 2026-10-20), and, by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z), "those things are single issue, you can directly mark the try to use serde-saphyr 2.x and don't need to mark a fixed date, now begin implementation, with fable do the orchestration and opus do the implementation", the G-base driver's step 8 on `serde-saphyr = "2.0"`, its comparison expecting serde-saphyr and granit-parser 1.3.0 → 2.0.0 (`96de1139`, 2026-10-20; programme plan §3.2 *G-base*, where rev 1.0 had left step 8's 1.3 unsettled); P30 itself is scheduled, not built. *Rev 22:* built — P30 (`f0fc0436`) writes `serde-saphyr = "2.0"` (2.0.0) with G-base step 8's options, C1's `emit_comments: false` and its pin test, C10's sentence, and A-07's fresh fixtures in its pin test and as E6's corpus; E6's `yamlcmp` refresh to this parser stays owed (programme plan §3.2 *P30*). |

#### Raised by `modernization-plan.md` rev 2.2 — M57's request-timeout retry (rev 4); taken (rev 7)

Not one of the review's D1–D13: modernization-plan rev 2.2 raised it while answering the review's SF-24 (M57's retry table) and left it for the owner (M57's entry, *The request-timeout question*). M57 lands once it is answered; its place in the order does not change. **Not adopted.** *Rev 7:* **taken (a)** by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z) (modernization-plan rev 2.5).

| Question | Options | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| Whether the router's key layer retries a request that ran out its whole `request_timeout`. Today one predicate, `LlmError::is_transient()` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/error.rs:101-102`, its doc comment "worth retrying with the same key"), decides the key ladder (`routing/router/retry.rs:203`), the fallback chain (`completion.rs:190`) and the agentic loop (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1435`); once M57 classifies real reqwest timeouts as transient, the key ladder would try such a request `max(pool.len(), 3)` times before the chain is reached | **(a)** The key layer does not retry it: a key-layer predicate beside `is_transient()` guards `retry.rs:203`, `is_transient()` stays true, so the chain and the loop still take the timeout, and the doc comment is narrowed to say so. **(b)** The key layer retries it like a connect timeout — the documented intent, one predicate | **Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)**, the owner's blanket answer: (a) — a key-layer predicate beside `is_transient()` guards the key ladder, `is_transient()` stays true so the chain and the loop still take the timeout, and the doc comment is narrowed to say so. Work: M57, §6.3 step 3, its place unchanged (modernization-plan rev 2.5). *As recorded:* (a): a server silent for the whole `request_timeout` is not helped by another key, and (b) multiplies the configured timeout by three or more before the chain |

#### Raised by the cut-day compatibility report — C5's reading of DU-23 for transitive JS (rev 6); taken (rev 7)

`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md` (rev 1) C5 found that `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge = 604800` counts seven days from the moment of install, while DU-23 counts from the cut instant: the direct JS pins are exact, but every transitive package floats in its range, so an install after the cut admits transitive releases published after 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z (undici 8.12.2 and @asamuzakjp/css-color 7.1.6 on 2026-10-17, report §3.4). Its fix — a `lockdates.py --pins npm --max-date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z` check over every `bun.lock` entry after each JS lock write (P31, P32, P33, P31a's JS half, M66, M67, M69), or each install run with `minimumReleaseAge` set to the instant — applies DU-23 as plan-rev3 rev 3.13 worded it for cargo; the report leaves the other reading, (b) below, to the owner, "not a default to adopt". **The bunfig reading (b) is not adopted**; (a) is DU-23 as written, which plan-rev3 rev 3.15's §5.2 already applies. *Rev 7:* **taken (a)** by the owner's blanket answer: the cut instant governs every lock entry, transitive JS included.

| Question | Options | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| Does the cut instant govern transitive JS packages, or `bunfig.toml`'s install-time seven days? | **(a)** The cut instant governs every lock entry, transitive JS included: C5's check after each JS lock write. **(b)** `bunfig.toml`'s install-time rule governs transitive JS; only the direct pins are held to the cut instant. | **Taken 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z)**, the owner's blanket answer: (a) — the cut instant governs every `bun.lock` entry, transitive JS included: C5's check after each JS lock write. Work: none new — plan-rev3 §5.2's `bun.lock` check and the G-base driver's `lockdates.py --bun-lock` step already apply it. *As recorded:* The report's fix (C5) is (a), DU-23 as written; it leaves (b) to the owner as a reading, not a default. What applies until the owner reads otherwise is (a): plan-rev3 rev 3.15's §5.2 `bun.lock` check, which says it "applies DU-23 as written" and names reading (b) as the owner's, and the G-base driver's `lockdates.py --bun-lock` step. *Rev 11:* for a JS lock write after P38 — M66, M67 and M69 — C5's check takes the commit's own instant minus 7 days as its max date, not the cut's (D1's *Rev 11:* note: the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 as the lead read it on 2026-10-20); through P38 the cut instant governs as taken. Nothing to build. *Rev 22:* applied at P31, P32 and P33 (`1302c5b6`, `093966fd`, `0286a80f`): `lockdates.py --bun-lock` over the v2 lock after each write, 0 too young with P31's undici 8.12.2 waiver and 1 without it. |

P04 (`e65b4eaa`, 2026-10-28; programme plan §3.2 *P04*) refreshed `Cargo.lock` within semver on the max date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z, and its gate's cargo-audit 0.22.2 (RustSec `99670cfc`) left 13 vulnerabilities: eight with named commits (lopdf P15, rmcp P19, quick-xml P16, zip's three at P16 then P34's ignores, zstd 0.13.3's with P16's hold lift), three fixed only in releases published after the max date, and two with no fix within semver; and the parent's clipboard-win 5.4.1 was yanked after the run of record. The first row is the owner's answer of 2026-10-27; the other two wait on the owner, each with the lead's recommendation (none adopted). *Rev 16:* the other two are the owner's answers of 2026-10-28 (PDT; 2026-10-28T13:54:23Z), each built — by P04c and P04d. The facts are P04's and P04b's gate records, read 2026-10-28 (UTC). *Rev 19:* P15 (`5c90a90f`) cleared lopdf's, and P16 (`19b5ded9`) quick-xml's two and zstd's with the hold lift; zip's three now match calamine's zip 8.6.0 alone. *Rev 20:* P19 (`93463cc6`) cleared rmcp's (RUSTSEC-2026-0189): cargo-audit 3, cargo-deny 10.

## 5. Recorded residue (not built)

One line each; the full text is in storage design §18 (by stage and subsection) and in each cited source. §18 holds 4
items from Stage 0, 31 from Stage 1 and 46 from Stage 2 (numbered here in §18's order), besides its opening paragraph
(sandbox research and merging projects, §4.6; T35, §1.1; R43, §3.3; T36 and ST-23, taken); every item is below, in §3, or
in the closed line further down.

**Storage design — Stage 0** (§18 Stage 0)
- `ToolContext`'s doc omits `prompt_ctx/sources/memory.rs` among `request_workspace_root`'s readers; the field collapse at `tools/registry/mod.rs:27-28` owns it.
- A lead-queued follow-up is pinned to the lane's active session, not its run's (see T18). *Rev 7:* T18 taken — the pin to `task.session_id` now; not built; until built, still met.

**Projects and registry** (§18 Stage 1 *Registry*; Stage 2 item 16)
- A control snapshot whose project id names no registered folder is skipped by rebuild and doctor, unlisted (R-S11).
- R11 is kept by `list` and `by_id`, not by `lookup`; two folders with one id confuse a re-point or delete.
- A stranded `projects/.<name>.<pid>-<n>.tmp/` after a crash in `register` is never swept (R-S14).
- No test of a case-sensitive project volume beside a case-insensitive home.
- With the home store outside `$HOME`, a `~/.git` makes `$HOME` a project (R-F7).
- A root that is not valid UTF-8 can alias another (Linux).
- An absolute path whose missing tail ends in `..` is refused rather than resolved to no project.
- While `projects/` cannot be listed every workspace route answers `500 IO_ERROR`; the error arm has no route-level test.
- A root asked for in another case or Unicode normalisation than recorded is resolved by its canonical form (R-T29's remainder).

**Re-point** (§18 Stage 1 *The re-point*)
- After a live re-point fails past step 2, both roots look up as nothing until the next boot.
- Recovery before step 3 re-runs `name_for` instead of reading the name step 2 wrote.

**Memory and learning, storage side** (§18 Stage 1 *Memory and learning*)
- A memory write for a root no folder records is refused before it takes a slot; the `unregistered:` slot is left only to keep two such writes apart, and a failed lookup takes it with a WARN.
- `root_gone` reads a failed lookup as "not gone", spending a model call per learning retry while `projects/` is unreadable.
- Task extraction across a re-point or a delete can lose a run's items (R-F4's remainder).
- A re-point between the gateway and the handler leaves the turn with no project (review R7's window).
- `POST /v1/memories` answers a stale root `500 MEMORY_INTERNAL`, not a typed 409.
- A deleted conversation's summary in a third project's partition stays until forgotten by hand.
- A project delete publishes no `memory_changed`; another window's Memory view keeps the deleted notes.

**Project delete** (§18 Stage 1 *The delete*; Stage 2 item 6)
- An in-flight chat turn is not refused; a project only an earlier build's rows name cannot be deleted; work runs inside the async handler.
- Review R16's and R3's windows: a put landing after the commit leaves a row of the deleted project; a late entry leaves a folder no list shows.
- Kept files' edges: empty `kept/` folders, NFD or case-folded targets refused, a crash-cut kept copy adopted, a permanent `500 DB_ERROR` for a taken address.
- Nothing tells "the folder was kept" from "it was already gone" (R-F2).
- The delete card counts no result-file rows (needs a wire change).

**The app and the CLI** (§18 Stage 1 *The app and the CLI*; Stage 2 items 39–41; live report)
- `openalpaca memory --project` reads the registry itself, so a CLI and daemon on different home stores can disagree (X9).
- `store rebase --dry-run` advises "delete that project's data first" where the daemon answers a 409 naming the restart.
- The CLI prints "1 partitions", "1 uploads".
- The Projects list: no registration event, roots drawn unescaped, no typed word on Delete data (R-S10; T33 stayed open until rev 7, taken 2026-10-17 as no typed word, so the dialog stays as built).
